I've even started avoiding travel entirely because the thought of being far from medical care is terrifying.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have distressing memories, nightmares, or flashbacks of the highway scare that make you feel as if it is happening again?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I frequently wake up in the middle of the night from vivid nightmares where I'm back in that car, gasping and unable to breathe. watching someone else’s life and can’t connect to any of the good parts.\n- S077 (Irritability_Aggression): I find myself snapping at my team and at home over the smallest things—someone being five minutes late to a Zoom, my husband asking if I’ve taken my meds—and I go from zero to furious so fast that afterwards I’m almost shocked at the harsh, biting tone that came out of my mouth.\n- S079 I frequently wake up in the middle of the night from vivid nightmares where I'm back in that car, gasping and unable to breathe. My chest tightens, my palms sweat, and I have this overwhelming urge to just bolt out of whatever room I'm in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any symptoms like being jumpy or easily startled, trouble sleeping, feeling constantly on guard, or avoiding thoughts and places that remind you of the highway scare?\n\nPatient: Yes, my sleep is terrible—I lie awake replaying that night or wake up gasping from nightmares of being trapped in the car, and I'm constantly on guard, jumping at sudden noises and checking for nearby hospitals wherever I go. I've also been avoiding travel entirely, even canceling a trip to visit my parents for Navratri because being far from medical care feels completely unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been steadily improving, or are they still going strong without much change? Since that night, it’s like a switch flipped—I’m going through the motions of my life and work, but I can’t feel any real joy or satisfaction in anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Since that highway scare, have you had repeated sudden episodes of intense fear or physical symptoms like a racing heart, shortness of breath, chest pain, or dizziness that peak within minutes? Or has it mainly been a persistent low mood and loss of interest since the event?\n\nPatient: It is actually both. I have these sudden, terrifying moments—even during board meetings—where I feel pulled right back to that highway, my chest tightens, my palms sweat, and I want to bolt or call 911. But alongside those rushes, there is also this constant, heavy flatness where I just can't feel any joy or connection to the good things in my life anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long has this been going on since the highway scare—has it lasted less than a month, or has it continued for more than a month?\n\nPatient: It has been going on continuously for about three weeks now, so it has been less than a month. It started that very night on the highway and hasn't really let up since then.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been mainly avoiding reminders of the highway scare and feeling on edge because of that event, or have you also had unexpected panic attacks that seem to come out of nowhere even when nothing reminds you of it?\n\nPatient: They are deeply tied to that scare; even when the panic seems to strike out of nowhere while I'm brushing my teeth or in a meeting, I suddenly feel right back on that dark road clutching the steering wheel. Because of this, I am constantly on edge, monitoring every flutter in my chest and checking where the nearest hospitals are. I've even started avoiding travel entirely because the thought of being far from medical care is terrifying.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have distressing memories, nightmares, or flashbacks of the highway scare that make you feel as if it is happening again?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I frequently wake up in the middle of the night from vivid nightmares where I'm back in that car, gasping and unable to breathe. During the day, even in board meetings, I'll suddenly feel transported right back to that dark road, clutching the steering wheel with my chest tightening and palms sweating as if it's happening all over again.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you ever feel detached from yourself or your surroundings, or feel like you are reliving the highway event as if it is happening right now?\n\nPatient: Yes, I absolutely feel like I am reliving it. In those moments, I suddenly feel like I am right back on that dark stretch of road, clutching the steering wheel and convinced I'm about to die. My chest tightens, my palms sweat, and I have this overwhelming urge to just bolt out of whatever room I'm in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any symptoms like being jumpy or easily startled, trouble sleeping, feeling constantly on guard, or avoiding thoughts and places that remind you of the highway scare?\n\nPatient: Yes, my sleep is terrible—I lie awake replaying that night or wake up gasping from nightmares of being trapped in the car, and I'm constantly on guard, jumping at sudden noises and checking for nearby hospitals wherever I go. I've also been avoiding travel entirely, even canceling a trip to visit my parents for Navratri because being far from medical care feels completely unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been steadily improving, or are they still going strong without much change? Also, have they lasted longer than one month yet?\n\nPatient: They haven't improved at all—they are still going strong and haven't let up since that night. It has been about three weeks since the scare on the highway, so it hasn't quite been a full month yet.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms lasted for less than one month, or have they continued for more than one month?"
  }

It has been less than a month. It has been exactly three weeks since the scare on the highway, and these symptoms have been continuous ever since.

During the day, even in board meetings, I'll suddenly feel transported right back to that dark road, clutching the steering wheel with my chest tightening and palms sweating as if it's happening all over again.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen, do you ever feel detached from yourself or your surroundings, or feel like you are reliving the highway event as if it is happening right now?\n\nPatient: Yes, I absolutely feel like I am reliving it. In those moments, I suddenly feel like I am right back on that dark stretch of road, clutching the steering wheel and convinced I'm about to die. My chest tightens, my palms sweat, and I have this overwhelming urge to just bolt out of whatever room I'm in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any symptoms like being jumpy or easily startled, trouble sleeping, feeling constantly on guard, or avoiding thoughts and places that remind you of the highway scare?\n\nPatient: Yes, my sleep is terrible—I lie awake replaying that night or wake up gasping from nightmares of being trapped in the car, and I'm constantly on guard, jumping at sudden noises and checking for nearby hospitals wherever I go. I've also been avoiding travel entirely, even canceling a trip to visit my parents for Navratri because being far from medical care feels completely unbearable.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been steadily improving, or are they still going strong without much change? Also, have they lasted longer than one month yet?\n\nPatient: They haven't improved at all—they are still going strong and haven't let up since that night. It has been about three weeks since the scare on the highway, so it hasn't quite been a full month yet.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms lasted for less than one month, or have they continued for more than one month?\n\nPatient: It has been less than a month. It has been exactly three weeks since the scare on the highway, and these symptoms have been continuous ever since.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for less than one month, or have they continued for more than one month?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has definitely been less than a month. It has been about three weeks since the incident on the highway, and I've been feeling this way every single day since then.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms continued for more than one month since the highway scare, or are they still within the first month?\n\nPatient: They are still within the first month. It has been exactly three weeks since the scare on the highway, so it hasn't reached a full month yet."

A panic-spectrum condition remains in the differential because there are recurrent sudden surges of fear with autonomic symptoms, but these episodes are clearly tied to the traumatic event rather than occurring as fully unexpected attacks. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression. Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
